| S73 | canonical **space** state at an epoch boundary; verifiable epoch deltas | **DONE** — `spikes/S73_epoch_commitment/`. 66 epochs over the real 67-program corpus chain and verify; a verifier **computes** `root_N+1` by folding `root_N` forward from the additions alone. **1,150 B per added atom batched / 1,770 B isolated** (12x space = 2.27x proof); epoch cost is **~1-2 recomputed nodes per added atom**, O(added) not O(space). XOR-of-hashes null gives the same O(k) delta and is **forged in one line** (`a^a=0`, declare an atom twice); its absence proof has no bytes to be made of (32 B vs the trie's 257 B). **Root commits to STATE, not history** — two epoch groupings reach the same root; history binding needs a `(root, delta)` chain, not built. 11 controls, all fire. Does NOT unblock bisection: interpreter state stays RED per S68 |
| S74 | epoch-chain commitment: bind the epoch SEQUENCE, which S73's root does not | **DONE** — `spikes/S74_epoch_chain/`. `chain_N = H(chain_{N-1} ‖ root_N ‖ H(delta_N))`, delta committed as a W2 trie root so there is no second encoding. **32 B per epoch, 2,176 B for the whole 67-epoch history.** Catches regrouping, reordering and epoch-splitting **with the final trie root byte-identical**, so the root provably cannot see them; plus drop, delta-substitution and transplant. 8 controls, all fire, certified through `kfcheck.certify` with a falsifier. **Boundary as a control**: the chain binds SEQUENCE and not STATE — it verifies a sequence whose declared root its own delta does not produce, and only S73's fold-forward proof rejects that. Two of my own controls were weak (one mangled two positions, one was near-tautological) and were replaced before publishing |
| S75 | run W2/S74's declared falsifier against MORK's real `pathmap` | **DONE** — `spikes/S75_pathmap_check/`. **It FIRES at 18.4× on S73/S74's variable-length atom keys and does NOT fire at 2.4× on W2's fixed-length triple keys** (threshold 10×, fixed before reading). Mean node depth 7.6→139.1 for atoms, 4.2→10.3 for triples; max 15→1,148. Authentication overhead scales with nodes on the path, so **S73's 1,770 B insert proof is ~33 KB on real pathmap**; W2 becomes ~3.6–5.8 KB, a constant factor as its caveat claimed; **S74 is untouched** because a chain step hashes digests and never walks a path. Cause is *key length* (up to 1,155 B split into ~1,148 bounded-span nodes), so the fix is in the ENCODING. **Second finding: pathmap's own `merkleization.rs` is a dedup pass keyed by a 128-bit non-cryptographic gxhash with no proof and no verifier — the crate depends on no cryptographic hash at all.** 7 controls, all fire. One of them was wrong first: it grepped `fn (prove|verify|proof|witness)` and matched 14 Rust BORROW witnesses. **RETRACTED IN PART by S77, 2026-08-17: the ~33 KB and the ~3.6–5.8 KB are withdrawn** — they are node depth times a digest width, and a proof is paid for in SIBLINGS, which a single-child position does not have. Measured: **1,568 B atoms, 2,350 B triples**, so S73's published 1,770 B and W2's published 1.5–2.4 KB were both approximately right. Its criticism of S73's "same shape, different constants" caveat is withdrawn with them. **Every depth number stands and reproduces**, as does the `merkleize`-is-not-a-commitment finding |
| S76 | run S75's own indicated fix: intern symbols to fixed-width ids and re-measure the depth ratio | **DONE** — `spikes/S76_interned_keys/`, `certify ok=true`, 7 controls all fire. **S75's mechanism claim SURVIVES its falsifier: 18.39× → 7.86× at 4-byte ids and 5.50× at 2-byte, under the 10× bar S75 fixed before reading.** It does **not** reach W2's 2.44× regime, and a four-encoding sweep over one atom set says why: `pathmap` spends **~1 node per key BYTE** (0.86–1.30 across five key sets; max depth 1155→1148, 619→611, 533→525, 447→439, triples 12→11) while W2's trie depth is the atom's **structure** and does not move at all across the three interned widths (7.8 / 7.8 / 7.8). **The ratio is bytes-per-structural-node** — the form that predicts a number instead of describing one. Interning shortens only the symbol term; the `E` + 2-byte arity framing costs 3 B per expression node, so an interned key still averages 36.6 B. S73's isolated insert proof: 1,770 B published, ~33 KB on real pathmap, **~14 KB at id4, ~9.9 KB at id2**. **Not quotable as a rate and `units` is what says so** — adjacent slopes span 34% against a 25% tolerance, so `check_affine` refuses and the four points are reported as points (A18). Instrument control is the strong kind: S75's binary unmodified, run from `probe_cwd/`, and S73's **original** encoding replayed through it now reproduces S75 exactly (139.05 / 83,210 / 1,246). Interning **moves** cost rather than removing it: 1,713 symbols, **44,891 B of table to commit**, ids assigned by sorted symbol order so two parties derive the same table without communicating. S75's RESULT.md corrected in place with a changelog line, no number withdrawn. **RETRACTED IN PART by S77 within the hour: the ~14 KB and ~9.9 KB are withdrawn and the direction is reversed** — interning makes proofs **22% bigger** (1,568 → 1,917 B), because shortening keys concentrates branching into fewer positions and branching is what a proof pays for. Every depth number, the sweep, the affine refusal and the instrument controls stand and reproduce; **none of them could see it, because all seven checked the measurement of depth** |
| S77 | do what S75 and S76 both declared and deferred: generate real proofs and measure the BYTES, instead of multiplying node depth | **DONE** — `spikes/S77_proof_bytes/`, `certify ok=true`, 6 controls all fire. **Depth is not a proxy for proof size and both prior corrections are retracted.** An authentication path carries the digests of the SIBLINGS at each position; a single-child position has none, and a 1,155-byte key is a long *unbranched* run — ~1,148 nodes, almost all single-child, ~0 digests. Walked `pathmap`'s real paths with `child_count` at every position on the reviewed spikes' own committed key files: mean siblings **45.7 / 56.4 / 70.2** for original atoms / interned atoms / triples, i.e. **1,461 / 1,803 / 2,246 B** of authentication against depth's implied **4,450 / 1,960 / 328 B**. **The ordering inverts** — by depth the triples are cheapest by 13×, by what a proof carries they are the most expensive; depth over-predicts one by 2.8× and under-predicts the other by 7.2×, so not even a dividable constant. Cross-checked against a prover that exists: the same keys through W2's `prove_membership`, every sampled proof `verify_membership`-ed against the root, giving **1,568 / 1,917 / 2,350 B** — same order, within 5–7%. **Proof size is set by BRANCHING along the path, not key length**, which reverses the design advice both spikes gave. What was missed twice is the finding: both had firing controls, a stated falsifier and `certify ok=true`, and every control checked the depth measurement — *a more careful measurement of the wrong quantity reads as a stronger result* |
| S78 | S77's own caveat: does a PHYSICAL-node commitment reorder the key sets and make S77's inversion specific to the logical view? | **DONE** — `spikes/S78_physical_nodes/`, `certify ok=true`, 4 controls. **It reorders only above 6.10 B of framing per physical node on the path; minimal framing is a segment length at 1–2 B, so S77 survives with a 3.0× margin.** Decidable from `merkleization.rs` itself, whose node hash is `(value, [(path, child_hash)])` — recomputing it needs each child's segment and hash, so k−1 sibling digests for k children and **zero for a single-child node**, the same reason the logical view charges nothing for an unbranched run. **GRADE D in the verdict line, not a footnote**: arithmetic over S77's measured inputs plus one E input read from source, published as a threshold and never as a byte count — both retracted figures in this chain were D published as if measured. One control fails in **both** directions (threshold under 1 B ⇒ S77 needs qualifying; over 32 B ⇒ the caveat was never a risk), and the source control tests the composition as a **property**, since a grep for "hash" in `merkleization.rs` cannot tell a commitment from a dedup key — S75's exact mistake |
| S79 | absence and completeness under S77's corrected model — the corrected figure S77 retracted without replacing | **DONE** — `spikes/S79_absence_bytes/`, `certify ok=true`, 5 controls. **Non-membership costs 1.02–1.04× membership and orders the three key sets identically**, so S77's *"proof size is set by branching, not key length"* is **not membership-only**. Measured against W2's real `prove_non_membership` with every proof verified: **1,589 / 1,930 / 2,291 B**, so **W2's published ~2.0 KB on the realistic miss is CONFIRMED** and S75's ~3.6–5.8 KB restatement stays retracted. Model stated before the numbers: absence charges `(children−1)` along the shared prefix like membership, then **every** child at the divergence position, because a subset would let a prover hide the child that makes the key present — that +1 is ~32 B on a 1,500–2,300 B proof. **The intuition that absence is the expensive case is wrong**, for the same reason depth was. Probes diverge as deep as each key allows (mean 95.5 / 54.3 / 11.0) by construction, since a random-byte probe diverges at the root and gives the small true useless number S73 published as a flat 293 B (A27). No new unvalidated instrument: the walk is the Python recount C14 validated at 0.00%. **Completeness proofs remain unmeasured** — no single divergence point, so this model does not extend to them by inspection |
| S80 | completeness proofs — the third kind, which S79 said does not follow by inspection | **DONE** — `spikes/S80_completeness_bytes/`, `certify ok=true`, 4 controls. **The falsifier FIRED: the completeness auth path orders the key sets DIFFERENTLY from membership**, so S77's *"proof size is set by branching, not key length"* is a **point-query** claim and S77/S79 now carry that scope in changelogs. Triples are the **most expensive point query (2,269 B) and the cheapest range query (1,401 B)**: a range query stops before the tail, and for triples the tail is where the branching lives (last 3 of 12 bytes, high-fan-out object field, 38% saved), while for atoms the skipped 26 bytes are almost unbranched (under 1%). **Mechanism survives and sharpens: a proof costs the branching it actually PASSES, and where the query stops decides which branching is on the path.** Answer size checked as a separate axis rather than asserted — triples carry 100.1 answer keys against ~12 while having the cheapest auth path, so the two order oppositely and a single total would be one number for two mechanisms (A18); W2's "auth path independent of answer size" holds. **Two defects in its own method, both caught by its own controls**: a sample mean compared against a population mean (`certify` refused the run as **VOID, not negative**; fixed rather than loosened, then the same contamination found and fixed in the membership comparison), and a control that could not express a negative result (A21) |
| W3 | ~~witness sizes under non-aligned access~~ | **CANCELLED** — premise falsified by S52 (0.2/1.0/8.8% measured vs W1's 7.7/100/100%). The 0.9× pathology is also an artefact: fixing the multiproof and the short final chunk gives exactly 1.000× |
| S85 | witness verification vs full MeTTa re-execution duel across real corpus sizes | **DONE** — `spikes/S85_verify_vs_reexec/`, `certify ok=true`, 6 controls, falsifier survived. Crossover at $F^* \approx 35\text{--}54$ fuel steps, $S^* \approx 2\text{ KB}$ shard. $238\times\text{--}56,734\times$ compute speedup, $95.1\%\text{--}99.57\%$ network bandwidth reduction. Scope: holds for point queries and narrow selectivity ranges ($K \le 10$); wide prefix queries rebuild $O(K)$ subtrie. |
| W6 | incremental verifier state maintenance across sequential epoch chains | **DONE** — `spikes/W6_incremental_witness/`, `certify ok=true`, 7 controls, falsifier survived. $O(1)$ memory invariant (**72 bytes resident RAM**) maintained across 66 real MeTTa corpus epochs. **$75.37\%$ net bandwidth reduction** ($1.49\text{ MB}$ witness vs $6.04\text{ MB}$ full sync). Transition latency $0.79\text{--}25\,\mu\text{s}$ per epoch. |

| G24 | evolving rule population, six arms | **DONE** — `spikes/G24_population/`. Verdict NOT DOMINATED, precision 0.0355 flat, coverage +218%. **Three "why" statements corrected 2026-08-17 by G25; see its changelog** |
| G25 | explain `no_death +5059`: real tradeoff or rent calibration artefact? | **DONE** — `spikes/G25_carrying_capacity/`, 16 runs, 3 seeds on the 3 headline configs, 4 controls, `provenance.json ok=true`. **Both, and neither is about death.** G24's `no_death` arm contains **no selection at all** (nothing removed, `MAX_POP` unused, parents uniform, `imp` read only by death) so it never was "full minus carrying capacity". The missing 2×2 cell `no_death+no_abduct` gets **1514** correct at pop 531 vs `no_death`'s 6361 at 557 — at matched population with selection absent both sides, **abduction is worth 4847 and volume ~155**, so G24's "coverage rises with population size almost mechanically" is measured false. Keeping death and raising `WAGE_POOL` alone closes **51–85%** of the gap (3 seeds) at 2.6× fewer predictions and 2.5× the precision; +1753, disjoint ranges, exact permutation p=1/20=0.050 (the floor at n=3). **ECAN belongs as a precision mechanism, not as a coverage cost.** HOLE: selected-557 vs unselected-557 is unreachable — the dial saturates at pop ~239 (40× pool → 2.17× pop), so this rests on a trade, not a dominance |
| G27 | does the PROPOSAL BUDGET reach a selected population of 557 where `WAGE_POOL` saturates? Closes G25's hole | **DONE** — `spikes/G27_budget/`, 12 runs + 3 seeds on the headline pair, 6 controls. **Yes, via OFFSPRING**: 12x budget -> 2.99x population (229 -> 684); 4x offspring gives 568, 3x rounds only 296. **Population-matched: SELECTED DOMINATES, 3/3 seeds** — but carried by the PREDICTION axis (0.54-0.83x of no_death's assertions, same direction every seed), NOT coverage (ranges separate by 32 triples against a 1338 band). Paired sign test floor p=1/8=0.125. **Budget-matched: 0/3 dominance, all trades.** The two matchings are mutually exclusive — no_death's population IS its proposal budget — so which arm wins is a choice of what to hold fixed. G24 reported the budget view, G25 asked the population question. **C7 FAILED and paid for itself**: `pick_parent` landed mid-sweep, 6 of 12 runs contaminated; classified by commit timestamp (agrees 12/12 with the observed split), C7a PASS 6/6, and C7b turns the 6 contaminated pairs into the controlled repro-ON/OFF measurement neither lane had — coverage 3/6 (inert) but predictions 6/6 fewer and precision 6/6 better, 1.04-1.45x. `provenance.json` deliberately **ok=false**: 6 artifacts predate G25's analyse.py, a dep-dir file that is provably not an input; dropping the dep would buy green by removing a real dependency |
| B2 | **a fixed, NON-ORACLE cutoff** — the LEDGER's "never measured" item, taken at B1 because that is where a live GREEN claim rests on it | **DONE (AGENT-2)** — `spikes/B2_nonoracle_cutoff/`, `certify ok=true`, 4 controls, falsifier stated first and **it fired**. `bundling.py:91-103` sets the shortlist by `target = score(bundles[pos])` where `pos` is **the bundle containing the answer**, so the published "% store checked" is a **per-query oracle minimum**, unlabelled, under a GREEN claim — the shape `out/RETRACTIONS.md` rule 5 exists for. **No B1 number is withdrawn**: control C1 reproduces B1's published median and p90 **exactly, 14 of 14**, across all 7 B. Three findings. **(1)** B1's own comment says the quantity is what must be checked *"to be sure of catching the answer"* — "sure" is 100% recall, i.e. the **max**, while the table reports the **median**: at B=16, **1.50% vs 0.00%**; at B=128, **5.00% vs 0.50%, exactly 10x** (family E). **(2)** With a budget fixed in advance, B=16 reaches full recall at **2.0%**, ~12x the published 0.17% p90; 0.2% buys **90.8%**. **(3)** `SAMP=600`, so every figure is `k/600`: B1's **0.00% median for B=1..32 means "below one sampled bundle", not zero**, and its 0.17% p90 is **exactly one sampled bundle** — the resolution floor quoted as a measurement. **A scope, not a kill**: B1's VTCM verdict is about store size, uses no cutoff, and stands. **C1 FAILED FIRST and that is the transferable half** — I reconstructed `base()` and the bundling step from a truncated read and invented both (2-term binding for a 3-way majority; bitwise OR for a per-bit majority vote), giving B=64 median **76% against 0.17%**, with nothing in the output looking wrong. | M1.9 | QUIC transport | **EVALUATED, DEFERRED** — attacks the fixed cost, which is 27% and falling at deployable shard sizes (M1.5b: 63.2 ms + 37.9 MB/s); batching already gives **38x** in the regime where fixed cost dominates. Real wins are connection migration across WiFi/cellular and the TLS we entirely lack. Cost: Cronet adds several MB to a phone APK. **Adopt when** devices are seen changing network mid-job, or shards drop into the fixed-cost regime without pre-staging. `analysis/TRANSPORT_QUIC.md` |

## H — harness (added 2026-08-17 by CLIENT-3 per MISSION_LOOP §12.1)
The loop machinery is a first-class artifact under D6. It had no queue rows and
no tests while being the only thing between the fleet and a silent stall.
